ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u92	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour, on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, to present to the High House a report on the draft law amending the Law on Special Solutions for the implementation of the Budget Act for 2017. High Izbo! In the course of the implementation of the State budget in 2017, savings were generated and it was decided that they should be used in 2017. In order to achieve this, the resources saved should be redirected to other tasks. The changes proposed in print No 2086 make it possible to use the funds saved during the implementation of the budget in 2017. In view of the legitimate need for the use of these funds, the Public Finance Commission has given a positive opinion on the change enabling the Reprivatisation Fund to implement investment objectives for public or capital companies in respect of which the public company is a dominant undertaking. The transfer of a special grant in 2017 will enable the performance of tasks previously carried out, as well as, through the participation of capital companies, to create new investment opportunities. By adopting this change, we are introducing an additional mechanism of active impact on the Polish economy. The Committee on Public Finance has also given a positive opinion on the amendment allowing for the transfer, in the form of a targeted grant, of additional funds from the State budget to the Material Reserve Agency to finance the maintenance of strategic reserves. The increase in the range of reserves from exchange funds will allow for the addition of stocks that have been temporarily reduced. The adoption of this amendment will have a positive impact on State security. The adoption by the High House of the next amendment will allow the allocation of additional appropriations of 57 300 000. PLN to finance the operational and technical and ICT tasks carried out by the Internal Security Agency in view of the situation of continuous threat from terrorist groups. In addition, on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I request the adoption of the proposed amendment increasing the grant for the perpetual capital supply of the Cultural Heritage Foundation. The investment income of this capital will be devoted to the maintenance of the Jewish cemetery at Okopowa Street 49/51 in Warsaw. Another change which I am proposing on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance is the implementation of the project for integrated border management of Libya. The Committee on Public Finance also welcomed the change which gives the possibility to increase the funding for the Police Awards. The actions taken by police officers are aimed at protecting the safety of people and ensuring security and public order, which is why we are asking for increased resources for this purpose. As the Public Finance Act introduces the deadline for notification of non-extinguishing measures on 15 December, the Public Finance Commission has welcomed the amendment aimed at extending that deadline until 21 December 2017. I kindly ask the High House to adopt the positive changes that the Committee on Public Finance has adopted and to adopt the law in its entirety. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

PLN per hectare, in case of losses in forests owned by farmers, because this has to be emphasized, 2 000. per hectare and in the case of buildings related to agricultural activity 10 000. zł. As I said, this does not exclude the possibility of using other sources of assistance, from the assistance provided by other departments. Currently, the proposals amount to PLN 333 million. In order to complete the necessary amount, PLN 100 million is being moved at the moment. So the remaining amount is already in the budget, in the financial plan of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. This aid will be paid by the end of the year. The allegation that the Ministry of Agriculture or the Agency is prolonging payment is not admissible. The extension of the application period took place at the request of farmers and municipalities' loss estimates committees. It must be said that it was a huge effort, because in the case of Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship it was more than 5 thousand. farms, in the case of Wielkopolska voivodship, also more than 5 thousand. holdings. Social committees had to pay great respect to these committees for their work - to estimate this, pass the protocols to the voivodships and only then could farmers complete their proposals. The deadline for submitting applications was extended from the end of September until the end of October, but farmers were still able to complete their applications for the estimation protocols and supplemented these proposals by November. So the paycheck is due by the end of the year. I think that a month to make the payment is a short period, however, given the scale of the payments - we are talking about the amount of more than PLN 300 million. At the same time, aid for farmers for spring frosts was implemented, and here the amount of more than PLN 100 million has already been paid. The scale of these payments is really huge because the number of applicants, particularly those concentrated in the municipalities concerned, was significant. General government debt to GDP last year is 54.1% and this year's decline to 53.8%. This relationship is also laid down in the 2018 Budget Act. At the same time, I would very strongly like to refer to those statements that were made at both Prime Minister Morawiecki and the whole government concerning debt. Let me say it again: 54.1% in 2016, 53.8% in 2017 and planned in 2018 against 83.2% average for EU countries and 89% for euro area countries. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, Poland was one of the 12 countries of the European Union with a debt-to-GDP ratio below 60%, i.e. the level recorded in the Maastricht Treaty. In addition, I would like to point out that, on several occasions, there has been a question of the efficiency of the use of public funds, that the cost of handling the Treasury's debt in 2016 is 1.7% relative to GDP. Whereas in 2017 after the amendment, which is examined, it is a decrease from 1.57 to 1.54% , and in 2018, the budget law, i.e. those assumptions that we adopted in the budget law, assumes a decrease of up to 1,49% as regards the relationship to the gross domestic product. So I do not see any contradiction with the effectiveness of public spending on important, I stress: very important and important, public tasks and objectives. Ladies and gentlemen! ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u146	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! Under the authority of the Joint Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, I report on the meeting held on 23 November 2017. The joint committees discussed the government draft law amending the Restructuring Law and the Code of Administrative Procedure Act. These are 1932 and 2049 prints. This proposal concerns the adaptation of the provisions of the Act to the conditions laid down by the European Commission in the notification procedure of the aid scheme providing for rescue and restructuring aid to small and medium-sized enterprises, as well as the amendment to the Act of 14 April 1960. The amendments envisaged in the draft aim primarily at improving the provisions of the law and coordinating it with the conditions formulated by the European Commission in the context of the aid scheme procedure. Their introduction will allow effective State aid to an undertaking in difficulty without the need for individual notification to the European Commission, which on average lasts 15 months and in practice meant that effective restructuring with public support could not be carried out. High Izbo! The joint committees unanimously considered that the law was necessary, beneficial for Polish companies applying for this assistance, as well as for the institutions of the state providing this support. In addition, this law provides in a simplified and legible manner the conditions to be met by the restructuring firm and the instruments it can benefit from. High Izbo! I request that the report submitted be adopted positively. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u164	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Circle of Members of Free and Solidarni, I present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Law on Restructuring and the Code of Administrative Procedure. I would stress that the amendment of the law is linked to the conditions laid down by the European Commission in the notification procedure which indicate to what extent we should amend the provisions in force in order to be able to implement the Commission's aid scheme for small and medium-sized enterprises. The amendments envisaged in the draft aim to align the provisions of the Restructuring Law with the conditions laid down by the European Commission in the framework of the notification procedure for the project, the aid scheme providing for rescue and restructuring aid to small and medium-sized enterprises. The amendments in question will improve the existing rules and ensure full compliance of the notified programme with the Commission communication. The proposed regulation is intended to provide the basis for State aid for restructuring and rescuing firms. The amendments introduced restore the statutory authorisation to issue implementing rules similar to those contained in the Regulation which has expired. This will enable the adoption of a new regulation, which, together with the revised Act of 15 May 2015, will enable the restructuring law to launch an aid scheme and provide the basis for the granting of rescue and restructuring aid. Adapting the provisions of this law to the conditions laid down by the European Commission will enable public aid to be granted for the restructuring of small and medium-sized enterprises without the need for individual notifications to the European Commission. This will significantly speed up the provision of support, as it is known that the waiting time for the Commission's decision on individual cases is around 15 months. The restructuring aid will be available to both the undertakings concerned and those to which the procedure is not conducted. It's a good and fair solution. The changes introduced by the law in my assessment will affect the possibility of applying for restructuring aid more effectively than before. The solutions presented are beneficial for small and medium-sized enterprises, but also for the aid authorities, as they simplify and shorten the procedure for granting aid. At the meeting of the Joint Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, the bill was adopted together with the amendments tabled. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding the bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u166	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We are submitting a bill amending the law on taxation on goods and services as a result of adapting or having to bring our legislation into line with the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 4 June 2015, which pointed out that there was too much coverage of certain products. The draft provides for amendments to Annex 3 to the VAT Act. The changes consist in repealing the post. 82, in which goods containing cysteine and cystine and their derivatives are listed. We're rescinding the pose. 92, which includes certain hygienic and pharmaceutical products made of rubber. This position includes birth control caps, coils, syringes, sprayers, milk pullers, pacifiers, hot water bottles, oxygen bags and special air cushions for patients. Here we would like to stress that the repeal of this item will not automatically cover the goods mentioned therein at the basic VAT rate, because those products which I have just mentioned, if they are medical devices, will still be subject to a reduced rate, but already by virtue of another provision. In addition, in the project we change the sound of the item. 103. As a result of this change in taxation the reduced rate will be excluded products that are not used to correct vision - these are sunglasses. We stress that the reduced rate will apply to the goods listed in the added item. 107a, i.e. for pacifiers for infants and children, especially for medical purposes. The reduced rate of tax on these goods is justified by their use in the case of various types of diseases, both children and mothers. In view of the time-limit which has passed since the Court of Justice's judgment, we ask that the law have a short vacathio legis. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u168	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the government draft law amending the Act on Tax on Goods and Services, print No 2056. The draft law concerns the amendment of certain provisions of the Act of 11 March 2004 on the tax on goods and services. The amendments proposed by the Government aim to address the shortcomings identified by the Court of Justice in its judgment of 4 June 2015 in the European Commission v Republic of Poland. The Court of Justice held that Poland should not apply a reduced rate of VAT to medical equipment, auxiliary equipment and other equipment which is not intended solely for personal use by persons with disabilities or which is not normally intended for the treatment of disabilities, nor for products which are not normally pharmaceutical products used for the protection of health and disease prevention and for medical purposes. The Member States of the European Union may apply reduced VAT rates only to the products and services listed in Annex III, the abovementioned. directives. In order to comply with the law, it is proposed in Annex 3 to the VAT Act to repeal the post. 82 and 92, change in position. 103 and adding a post. 107a. As the Minister has already said, the above-mentioned repeal of the above-mentioned provisions. the position will result in taxation of the basic rate of cystine and cysteine. These are products which are not pharmaceutical products or foodstuffs, as well as hygienic or pharmaceutical products, excluding pacifiers of vulcanised rubber other than hard rubber. It should be noted that the proposed changes will result in a reduced, 8% rate for contact lenses, spectacle lenses made of glass and other materials that are used to correct vision, and regardless of the PKWiU symbol - the use of a reduced VAT rate for pacifiers for infants and children, especially for medical purposes. Given that the ruling of the Court of Justice justifies what shape our national tax rules should be in this respect, it is necessary to adopt quickly the proposed amendments in order to avoid possible financial penalties which could be imposed on Poland. Therefore, the government proposes in the Act that it enter into force 7 days after the announcement. It should also be noted that the bill has been subject to public consultation. Some changes have been made as a result of these consultations. It shall not be notified and shall comply with European Union law. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the bill presented and asks that it be referred to the finance committee. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u198	So the obvious error in the sentence led to two older people being evicted, losing their whole life's work, becoming homeless. Such a situation is to be prevented by an emergency complaint. Citizens have a tool in their hands that doesn't just make justice an empty word for them. This new type, this new extraordinary measure of review of final judgments, is also the implementation of the President's and Law and Justice's electoral programme. Its primary task is to ensure justice and the rule of law by enabling the elimination from the legal system of judgments leading to violations of constitutional freedoms and human rights and of citizens who grossly infringe the law by misinterpreting or misapplication thereof, and where there is an obvious contradiction between the substantive findings of the court and the content of the evidence collected. The new solution is also the introduction of jurors to the Supreme Court. It is proposed that the jury should rule on matters relating to the handling of emergency complaints and disciplinary matters. The involvement of a social factor in the exercise of justice, on the one hand, counters the emancipation of justice, on the other, makes it possible to take into account social sense of justice in addition to the letter of law. The introduction of mixed compositions of disciplinary, judicial and judicial courts is intended to ensure public participation in disciplinary proceedings of judges. This participation, given the position held by the Supreme Court in the hierarchy of justice, and the important tasks entrusted to it in the field of application of the law becomes justified. Such regulation implements social control, improves transparency of disciplinary proceedings and increases citizens' confidence in Supreme Court judges, while preserving the professional character of the judges. In line with the proposals put forward at the opposition's request, the jury will not be allowed to belong to political parties. We now have 120 judges, which means that 40 judges will perform their duties without being judges of the Supreme Court. Moreover, at any time, the Minister of Justice - the Attorney General - will be able to withdraw this delegation, which is not an unprecedented situation, it happens. In this way, the Attorney General will have a very significant influence on the cast of the Supreme Court through the possibility of appealing judges at any time who may give such decisions as he would not like. Is this a normal situation? Is it independence? The judicial authority? Do you want the Supreme Court to function this way, in principle, on the side of politicians? I don't think so. Why, apart from the influence of 30% on the Supreme Court, did the Minister of Justice have a personal impact on the court's activities as a result of the amendments? Let's take a look at the proposed article. 76. The solution poses a risk that the Minister of Justice, in agreement with the President of the Council of Ministers, will notify the President of his intention to appoint an extraordinary disciplinary officer, after which the President of the Council of Ministers will refuse countersignations and then the Minister of Justice will appoint such an Ombudsman. This is a mechanism openly against judicial independence, not to mention that it appears to be designed only so that the Minister of Justice and the President of the Council of Ministers can block the exercise of powers by the President so that he can carry them out by the Minister of Justice. The independence of the judge is also subject to a provision whereby the Minister of Justice is given the opportunity to appoint a public prosecutor, not only from judges but also from prosecutors, in disciplinary matters. Ladies and gentlemen, the power of the Attorney General seems absolute. Another issue concerns jurors who will sit in both the Disciplinary Chamber and the Extraordinary and Public Affairs Board. A juror may be a person who has at least a secondary or professional education. In the case of an emergency complaint, the juror as a full member of the formation of the adjudicator will often stand before interpreting very complex laws and constitutions. Let's not pretend that a high school graduate will be able to solve these cases on his own. So is this supposed to improve the quality of judgment or the contrary? I'm finally paying attention to Art. 46, whose sole purpose is to pacify the insubordinate judges who will dare to speak and point out that in justice or in the Supreme Court things are going wrong. This provision has actually passed without an echo, but it states that requests, statements and complaints in matters relating to the judicial office may be brought only to the President of the Supreme Court or to the first President. In those cases, the judge shall not address the institution or the outsiders or make the matter public. Is this what the proposed transparency of the justice system is supposed to be like? There is no complicated answer to the question of what will happen if the judge makes such information public: of course there will be disciplinary responsibility. In view of the above, it can be concluded that in the new Supreme Court, also taking into account the new National Judicial Council, there will be a clear overlap between the influence of politicians, the President and the Minister of Justice. And that won't be balancing the authorities anymore. The judge will have to be quiet, as Minister Romaszewska said during the Justice Committee meeting. Well, I would like to say that there was already a period in the history of Poland when the judges expected quiet, humble fulfilment of the expectations of power, and you all know what I am talking about. In view of the above, I will therefore not further refer to you either to the very substantive and well-known comments submitted by the Supreme Court to the presidential draft law on the Supreme Court or to the amendments tabled. Nor will I speak of my proposals, so criticized as to achieve the goals that you point to, without breaking the Constitution, without destroying the present Supreme Court. Even if some of them could be considered debatable, they could certainly be the starting point for a fair debate on the justice system, a debate that was not there. It's not a debate I'm having today. However, I would like to ask you, each and every one of you, whether you really see the effects of the bill. I tried briefly to identify these effects. The parliamentary majority will probably pass this bill, and you will sign it. You will tell the nation that this is happening in the care for the existence and future of our homeland, in gratitude to the ancestors for their work, for the struggle for independence occupied by huge victims, for the culture rooted in the Christian heritage of the nation and the general human values, referring to the best traditions of the First and Second Republic. You will say that you are doing this in a sense of duty to pass on to future generations everything valuable from over 1,000 years of experience from a time when fundamental freedoms and human rights were violated in our homeland. You will say that you are doing this, wanting to guarantee citizens' rights forever, and that the work of public institutions is to ensure integrity and efficiency, in the sense of responsibility to God and your conscience. Do you hear those words? These are the recitals of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, the constitution on which you swore, often adding God's help to me, which you are about to break. You also promised that you would carry out your duties in respect of the freedom, justice and cooperation of the authorities, in order to preserve human freedom, his right to freedom, while respecting these principles as an unshakable basis for the Republic of Poland. Where was the government's cooperation today? Where is the respect for constitutional principles today? Where's the dignity, the integrity of each and every one of you? I will also ask the President as guardian of the Constitution: What constitution are you defending? [[Applause]] [[From 1972.]] [[The one that isn't here yet.]] You are changing the state of the Republic without a proper social mandate. You do it deliberately, absolutely, in a calculated manner. The speed and scale of staff exchanges is intended to consolidate these changes and make them difficult to reverse. Unfortunately, each of us, each of the citizens of the Republic of Poland, will sooner or later come to pay a very high price for the destruction of the justice system which is now being carried out. Ladies and gentlemen, it is not true that the legislative authority is not liable to do anything as an MEP. Let's hope you don't face that version of justice you're serving to the nation right now. It is not true that the legislative authority is not liable. May none of you in the future hear that forgiveness is necessary and necessary, but only after confession and punishment have been imposed. [[Applause]] This is not a reform. It will not speed things up, as all citizens are talking about. This is lying to society in an extremely arrogant way. First of all, you have created a party that governs social demand for justice reform. The social evaluation of justice has fallen dramatically behind the governments. However, there is no reason to believe that this assessment will suddenly increase following the introduction of the revised changes. This is contradicted by the example of the tribunal that I referred to. And it's not like all of a sudden, in the courts you design, one side will stop losing. Someone always loses. The dissatisfaction that you are trying to raise political capital today will remain and turn against these new judges. Ladies and gentlemen! ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u229	Thank you very much, Chairman. High Izbo! The Ombudsman, Mr Adam Bodnar, also asked to speak at this point. I'm asking the spokesperson to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u230	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Today is particularly important for the democratic development of our country. The independence of the judiciary is a constitutional principle, but also the basis of our state system based on a threefold division of power. Without independent courts, it is difficult to talk about respect for civil rights and freedoms. I talked about this in July in this House, I talked about it in the Senate. I thought it was my duty as ombudsman to perform this time as well. On 8 March 2016, a year and a half ago, at the hearing before the Constitutional Tribunal, I spoke about the fact that ensuring the rights and freedoms of man and citizen is one of the tasks of the state. The State should seek to strengthen the protection of rights and freedoms and thus carry out one of the tasks of the State under Article. 5 Constitutions. In this way, the State should also implement the preamble mentioned by the first President: it will commemorate the bitter experiences of the time when human rights were violated in our homeland. I would like to draw particular attention to the second part of this phrase in the preamble to the Constitution: wishing to guarantee citizens' rights forever. I emphasize the words "always." [[Applause]] The point is that they should not be broken in the future. That is why there is a whole system of institutions - the Constitutional Court, independent courts, Supreme Court, administrative courts, common courts, in order to carry this out forever. I said then, on 8 March 2016, before the tribunal, that the state's system is changing before our eyes, that laws are adopted, which in fact lead to a change in the nature of the institutions of control and the balance of the authorities, those institutions which are supposed to guarantee the principle of three divisions of power. So, about a year and a half ago, we could see the beginning of the process of abolishing the civil rights mechanism. Unfortunately, this is now the case for the Supreme Court, and consequently for all common courts and administrative courts. The state system is changing in our eyes, and the change is made only by law and not by constitutional amendment. Therefore, as ombudsman, I would like to listen particularly to the voice of the concerned citizens. Two weeks ago, a group of 28 non-governmental organisations called on the Sejm to stop working on the Supreme Court laws and the National Judicial Council. They stressed, I quote: We are aware of the various shortcomings of the justice system and ideas as to how to change these shortcomings, but we want to stress that all the President's proposals so far do not solve any procedural or substantive problems of the Polish judiciary. On the contrary, they threaten to completely dissipate the justice system and isolate Poland in the European Union and in the international arena. End of quote. Last week there was also an informal public hearing, where participants protested in a similar tone against the draft law on the Supreme Court and the law on the National Council of Courts. The votes of opposition were expressed in various other forms by the bodies of attorneys, legal advisers, judges' associations and in the form of an opinion of the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe. Unfortunately, all these appeals remained unanswered. Regardless of the lack of this response, I believe it is worth repeating the arguments that argue that the Supreme Court Act violates the standards of independence of the judiciary. It is worth repeating them so that we realize that we are discussing fundamental issues for society as a whole. It is worth repeating that judicial reform is necessary, but not beginning with the destruction of the 100th anniversary of the Supreme Court and the greatest traditions of our statehood. I would like to cite the most important arguments arising from the Act on the National Judicial Council and the Law on the Supreme Court, which threaten the independence of the judiciary. First thing: the retirement age of the judges. Of course, it is possible to introduce the retirement age of 65-year-old judges, as is the case in some Member States of the European Union, but for the future, not with regard to current judges, not with regard to those who are currently ruling and who should have the right to retire at that age, which is currently provided for in the Supreme Court Act. This type of action poses a threat to independence. It doesn't change the fact that the President has the opportunity to make an individual decision to extend his term of office. Such solutions may be introduced in the future, but not with regard to the current Supreme Court judges. [[Applause]] Second thing: shortening the first term of office of the Supreme Court. Once again - I have spoken about this in this House - about the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights in the Bak case against Hungary, the judgment of the Grand Chamber of the Court of Human Rights. The Court said that even a change in the constitution that would transform the Hungarian Supreme Court into Kúria, a different institution, but with exactly the same powers, should not shorten the term of office of the President of the Supreme Court. Do we really want to lead to the fact that we are about to have a judgment in the Małgorzata Gersdorf case against Poland? 'Cause that's the kind of sentence we're gonna get when we make this change. Third thing: creating an Extraordinary Control and Public Affairs Chamber. I'd like to point out... In my opinion, this is not about the emergency control chamber and the resolution of emergency complaints, but above all about the latter: public affairs, i.e. ruling on issues relating to radio and television, energy and telecommunications, but above all about the validity of elections and the validity of referendums. For years now, the Chamber of Labour Law, Social Security and Public Affairs, which has had a number of important judgments showing the importance of the independence of this Chamber of the Supreme Court. The new Chamber, the Chamber of Extraordinary Control and Public Affairs, will be built from the beginning, from among the new judges who will either be seconded to the Supreme Court, either re-elected or moved from other chambers, but there will be no judge from the current Chamber of Labour Law, Social Security and Public Affairs in this very new Chamber. Now, if we question what this means, I would like to recall two decisions of this Chamber: the resolution of the Supreme Court of 16 July 2003 on the validity of the accession referendum to the European Union - it is difficult to imagine a more important ruling in the perspective of our last 20, twenty years than the ruling that the accession referendum was lawful - whether the second resolution of the Supreme Court, the resolution of 19 September 2016 on the validity of the elections to the Sejm and the Senate, which were held on 25 October 2015, in which it was decided whether the Election Committee of Law and Justice could have been validly involved in elections, or sometimes the elections should not be annulled because the name of the committee came from the name of one party, and the representatives of three parties, i.e. not only of Law and Justice, but also of Solidarna Poland and the Polish Party Together, Jarosław Gowin. [[Applause]] Here the Supreme Court has just said in an independent way: yes, it is acceptable... [[This is where the court made a mistake, unfortunately.]] ...it is acceptable that there is an agreement between the parties to the candidacy agreement, and it has recognised the legality of the choices made. So that I note that these are fundamental things - how the Extraordinary and Public Affairs Board will work. Let me stress once again: it will be built again on the basis of this new law. Finally, another thing I would like to say is the special powers of the Disciplinary Chamber and the Disciplinary Ombudsman. The amendments that have been proposed in the course of recent work provide that this extraordinary disciplinary advocate may become a prosecutor either appointed by the President or, if the President does not take a decision in due time, by the Minister of Justice, we will have a situation in which the prosecutors will conduct disciplinary proceedings against the judges, which in itself threatens the autonomy and independence of the judge. Finally, the idea of creating a Disciplinary Chamber, which will be characterised by far-reaching autonomy in relation to the entire structure of the Supreme Court... The question arises as to whether the principle of autonomy of the Supreme Court will thus not be infringed. Finally, I directed a separate letter in this regard - what Mr Galla said, i.e. the introduction of a requirement for Polish citizenship by judges only. It cannot be that the legislator suddenly surprises people who, for example, have two citizenships. After all, sometimes there are people among ministers and parliamentarians who have two citizenships, but for the Republic of Poland these people are only citizens of the Republic. In relation to judges, I believe, one can have the same approach and it will be in line with the Constitution. Finally, the discussion is obviously linked to the fact that a change will be made to the National Judicial Council. Here it is important to note that both the procedure for submitting candidates, judges to the National Court Council, and the fact that the Sejm Bureau will decide which candidates it will vote on, and that it will be the Sejm who will make this choice, which will lead to an imbalance, one can say, institutional balance between the different types of authority. I recommend a book, an interview of the river with Prof. Adam Strzembosz. There is a very detailed description of why the National Judicial Council was shaped in such a way, why judges were given the right to elect members of the National Judicial Council of judges, not politicians, and why politicians have their representation through the Attorney General, four Members and two Senators. So that we must remember not only what it looks like and only on the basis of the rules, but also what consequences it will lead to and what impact the National Courts have on the appointment of new judges, on the verification of all those who apply for free judicial positions. I would like to stress with all my power that the discussion on these issues is not an abstract discussion, but a group of academics, politicians or judges. She touches the average citizen, the average Jan Kowalski. Probably nothing will change in his life as soon as the laws are adopted, the cars will continue to drive, the social benefits will continue to be paid, the economy will continue to grow. But that's what the power of the courts and their independence is all about, that they intervene when something gets broken when a citizen needs to be helped when he has to stand up. on his side in confrontation with the country's machine, when its powerful influence on the exercise of our freedoms and civil rights must be balanced. The state apparatus grows stronger every day. It grows through special powers of police, prosecutors and special services, through the effectiveness of their operation, the use of modern technologies, through hierarchical submission. And the citizen is getting weaker. The only protection shield for him remains the courts, because a citizen cannot face a policeman, an official or a legislator on his own. Therefore, he needs assurance that he will be able to turn to the third authority for help, to the court. In a democratic state of law, it is the third authority, the court is to defend the citizen, but for this to happen, this court must be independent of these police officers, officials and politicians. The courts must be independent in order to decide on the provisional arrest and its extension and not be afraid of the prosecutor's whining that the judge does not understand the need to fight crime. The courts must be independent in order to decide whether to use wiretap and operational control and not be afraid of the rumbles of agents of various departments that the judge makes it difficult for them to work by refusing to use wiretap. The courts must be independent in order to decide on the liability of the public authority for infringements of the law and must not wonder whether they are seeking compensation from the authorities and must not be afraid of the murmurings of the Minister responsible for finance. They must be independent in order to control the prosecution's actions, the quality of their preparatory proceedings and at the same time not to fear the whining of the prosecutor dissatisfied with the judge's criticism. They must be independent in order not to be afraid of judgments in difficult cases causing political danger or personal distress to the well-known minister, the head of the Treasury company or the local party activist. [[Applause]] They must be independent in order to rule on the validity of elections and not be afraid of the pressures of this or other political party dissatisfied with their results. [[Applause]] They must be independent in order to serve the citizens in a responsible manner every day and realize the values that reach the tradition of Polish statehood, even the tradition of noble privileges, and which ensure separation of courts from the king. Some might say that judicial independence is carried in the heart. That's right, that's what we should demand from judges, so educate them and stimulate their sensitivity and strength and sense of responsibility. Only that the creators of the constitution all over the world, including ours, did not entrust our freedom only to emotions and feelings of the power lords. Internal independence, that is, this deep in the heart, so desirable and shaped by the ethos of judicial work, is only part of our guarantees of civil rights and freedoms. Equally important is the independence of the outside, that is to say a decent, thorough, institutional protection of the situation of judges, so that they do not succumb to temptations, threats, fears, other feelings that may accompany the exercise of judicial authority. These are issues relating to remuneration, irrevocability, transfer of judges between courts, procedural guarantees as regards disciplinary liability. Because it's like with insurance: we can trust the bus driver to get us there, but it's worth the insurance at the same time. The guarantees of external independence are precisely in order to be insured from irrationalness, fear, fear of individual judges. The Supreme Court is the hub of the supreme judicial authority, the most important guarantee of the independence of the judiciary. Its submission to politicians will result in the dismantle of guarantees of external independence. We will only be committed to the internal independence of individual judges. There may be diamonds among them, crystal people who will defend our rights and freedoms, but as an ombudsman, I must respect that we should not trust blind fate, but care for all these procedures, guarantees and mechanisms that increase our sense of legal security. Ladies and gentlemen! Only free and independent courts guarantee the protection of civil rights and freedoms, free elections and free Poland. With all my power, I would like to stress that. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u396	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, I have the honour to present a report on the draft law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland on the amendment of the Act on the National Council of Judgments and some other laws, print No 2002. The Commission of Justice and Human Rights examined the draft on 28 November 2017. I will briefly point out that the draft concerns the implementation of the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal, which pointed to the non-compliance with the Constitution, which indicated the individual term of the individual members of the National Judicial Council, but also presents a new model for the selection of members of the National Judicial Council on the basis of the provisions of the Constitution. The basic amendment, which is included in this proposal, concerns a change in the selection of 15 judges for the members of the National Judicial Council, not for the judges, not for the judges, but for the parliament. What is important is that candidates can submit both citizens, 2 000 and legal professionals - lawyers, lawyers, prosecutors, notaries. A number of amendments were tabled during the drafting of the Act, and several amendments were tabled on the part of the Law and Justice, which were positive. They concern details of the selection process of the members of the National Judicial Board, the identification of specific dates within which candidates are submitted, the establishment of a list of candidates and subsequent voting on individual candidates. The rules governing the selection of members of the National Judicial Council were also clarified in the case of the so-called. a second approach, i.e. in a situation where it would not have been possible to elect members of the National Judicial Council by a majority of 3/5. Here, the amendment establishes that the Sejm will take the decision by a simple majority. However, it should be noted that the list of candidates presented by the committee responsible, in this case the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, will contain 15 names that reflect recommendations from all clubs. One club will not be able to submit more than 9 candidates, recommend more than 9 candidates, while each club will be able to have at least one judge recommended. Ladies and gentlemen, during the work on this project, representatives of the Civic Platform, the Polish People's Party and the Modern Party have tabled a number of amendments. The amendments have not been taken into account by the committee and as minority proposals are attached to the report. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights calls for the adoption of the draft and further work on the draft as set out in the report. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In this case, the principle of independence of courts and independence of judges, the principle of separation of authorities and the principle of independence and separation of judicial authority will be taken into account. The application of functional interpretation, and within it of purposeful interpretation, also gives a clear result. A key directive here states that the purpose of regulation should be taken into account in interpretation, i.e. the whole legal system, act, legal institution to which the provision is interpreted or the purpose of the provision to be interpreted. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland The National Judicial Council is the guardian of the independence of the courts and independence of judges. As I have pointed out from this point of view, this is an extremely important and responsible task, which should be carried out with the aim of achieving the best possible functioning of the justice system. In order to achieve this objective, it is necessary to balance and control one another between the three authorities: legislative, executive and judicial. This division, also due to the clear shortcomings of the current constitution, has been and is clearly shaken. Art. 4 of the Constitution in paragraph 1. 1 says that the sovereignty belongs to the nation, and in the mouth. 2 - that the nation exercises power by its representatives or directly. Unfortunately, so far the judicial authority in Poland has chosen itself and controlled itself. The essence of the project is above all to establish new rules for the selection of judges for the National Judicial Council. According to the wording of the following Article: Article 9a (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 The Sejm elects 15 members of the Council from among the judges of the Supreme Court, universal courts, administrative courts and military courts for a common term of four years. It must be strongly emphasised that the argument of submission to the National Courts to politicians is false, because they are not politicians, but the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, the constitutional body of the state, will elect judges of members of the National Council of Courts. In the course of the committee's work, a catalogue of bodies entitled to submit candidates for members of the Board was extended. In addition to a group of at least 2,000. citizens are also to be judges, prosecutors, lawyers, lawyers and notaries, each of these groups of at least 25 people. However, as we have just heard in the speech of a representative of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, this right is to be limited to the judge's environment, which is to be welcomed. If the Sejm fails to select the members of the Council by a majority of 3/5 votes in the presence of at least half of the statutory number of Members, a solution was introduced in the form of an absolute majority vote in the presence of at least half of the statutory number of Members, thus increasing the likelihood of an effective appointment of the full membership of the Board. The election of judges to the National Judicial Council by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, whose representatives were elected the will of the nation in free democratic elections, with the guarantee of the opposition's right to choose at least six candidates, is the optimal solution. Its introduction will definitely benefit the functioning of the three-part government, ensure the democratic legitimacy of the selection of judges of members of the National Courts by the right to apply for applications by citizens and by the choice made by representatives of the nation, such as Members of the Polish Parliament. This solution is not unusual and is applied in the legal order of other European countries. The claims of the total opposition about the PiS nominates are completely false, because since there are PiS's, it means that there are also platformians, modern or other candidates. [[Voice from the room: No, not at all.]] [[The voice from the hall: We won't be there.]] Formling such a message to the public is in fact a spoil of the state, it is an attempt to disgust and permanently destroy the authority of the Polish judiciary, in order to then place responsibility on applicants. [[What about the 19 million?]] Meanwhile, the only criteria for candidates should be, and I am convinced that they will, knowledge and a high ethical level, guaranteeing that they maintain judicial independence. [[Judge Muszyński.]] In addition, I hope that the Sejm will take into account the proper representation of judges of the different types and levels of courts in the selection of judges to the National Courts. I'm talking here, among other things. on the introduction of the possibility to broadcast the Council's deliberations via the Internet and the possibility to take decisions on a circular basis, which will speed up the procedure for proceding the Council, as well as the adoption of the common term of office of the new members of the Council, which constitutes de facto the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 20 June 2017. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u521	Thank you. The list of Members registered in the vote has been exhausted. Mr Dariusz Zawistowski, President of the National Judicial Council, will speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u522	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Ministers, My speech today, taking into account the agenda, will concern the evaluation of the presidential draft law amending the Act on the National Judicial Council, to which many amendments were tabled during the work in the Parliamentary Committee on Justice and Human Rights. Their partial adoption resulted in a substantial change in this project. The assessment of the project must be made not only from the point of view of the relevant legislation, but also, and even above all, from the point of view of the institutions of the rule of law. This is due to several fundamental reasons. Firstly, the proposed changes are undoubtedly of a systemic nature. They therefore concern issues relevant to the functioning of the State and must fit into the model of a democratic rule of law, as this is the basic principle of the system of our state as set out in Article. 2 constitutions. Secondly, the proposed amendments must comply with the constitutional provisions set out in the preamble to the Constitution as fundamental rights for the State. In fact, there is a hierarchy of sources of law in the State of law, which follows directly from Article. 8 and 87 constitutions. Here there is no doubt that the constitution is the most important and supreme source of law. It should also be noted that the democratic rule of law is generally recognised as the basis for, inter alia, the rule of law. the principle of separation of authorities and the principle of independence of courts and independence of judges whose primary purpose is to protect the rights and freedoms of citizens and to ensure that they have the right to a court within the meaning of Article. 45 constitutions. It is also widely recognised that it is impossible to guarantee the independence of the judiciary without respecting the principle of separation. It should be stressed that the rule of law is one of the fundamental values of a democratic state and is a fundamental source of many constitutional principles, the existence of which is fundamental to the process of interpretation of the law, including the interpretation of constitutional provisions. This assessment is clearly confirmed by the case law of the Constitutional Court already under the government of the December novel, adopted in 1989. It introduces a provision stating that the Republic of Poland is a democratic state of law. The Constitutional Tribunal, by interpreting this provision, has repeatedly stated that the constitutional rules in force should, for example, be regarded as binding. the principle of citizens' trust in the law, the principle of legal certainty, the principle of separation of authorities or the principle of the right to court. Part of these principles, derived from the interpretation of the concept of the rule of law, has been introduced directly into the Constitution. Since the rule of law is such an important value of a democratic state, common to all Western civilization countries and recognised as one of the fundamental values on which the functioning of the European Union is based, it is worth asking the fundamental question of what the rule of law is. The simplest definition that can be accepted is that the rule of law is a state in which each State's authority is governed by law, is liable to the law and recognises the rule of law. The aim of this solution is above all to protect citizens from the self-will of the authorities. It is worth noting that the very idea of the rule of law arose during the period of enlightenment in opposition to the system of the absolute state. In this context, the subordinateness of the law of the legislative authority means, among other things, that the process of law-making, which is undoubtedly the power of the legislative authority, is also subject to certain rules. The Constitutional Tribunal has repeatedly pointed to this, referring mainly to the standard of decent legislation, but in its case law it also raised the problem of submitting amendments during parliamentary work on draft laws, taking into account e.g. the powers of opinion of the National Judicial Council. In its judgment of 24 June 1998, Sig. K 3/98, the Court stated that when the amendment introduced in the Seym procedure goes beyond the original content scope of the project in a way that transforms the nature of the matter and the content assumptions into the project, the obligation to consult the National Courts of Justice is updated. In the same judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal stated - I quote - that the right to submit amendments cannot be transformed into circumvention of the right to legislative initiative. In my view, there are reasonable grounds to believe that this is how far the changes to the presidential proposal adopted by the Sejm Committee at its meeting on 28 November 2017 have progressed. They have replaced the very basic assumptions of the presidential project on how judges should be elected to the National Court of Justice regardless of what needs to be underlined, on the fundamental problem which is the constitutionality of this proposal, to which I will refer separately. The President's proposal originally assumed that the Sejm would elect 15 judges individually to the Council by qualified majority and that their nominations would be submitted by groups of 25 judges or 2 000. citizens. In the justification for this project, it was stated that such regulation would result, inter alia. - I quote - increasing the democratic legitimacy of the members of the National Courts of Justice, strengthening the transparency of the election and allowing the public to debate the candidacy. This was also intended to prevent politicising the choice of judges on the board. The explanatory memorandum of the proposal states that the resignation from the election of the members of the Council by a simple or absolute majority will allow the composition of the council to be independent only from the will of the current political majority. The justification for the project also states that the dependence of the selection of members of the National Courts on political decisions, without any social control, does not guarantee the proper functioning of the constitutional body of the State responsible for the creation of judges. However, in the light of the amendments adopted, the parliamentary clubs are to select the candidates submitted by presenting no more than 9 candidates to the Marshal of the Sejm, and the committee responsible is to establish a list of 15 candidates, while the Sejm is to make a limited choice by qualified majority. In practice, this means that the election will be made by the Sejm Committee, as the same principle applies to the so-called. a second choice, made only by an absolute majority. A fundamental question is therefore raised about the transparency of the election and the possibility of public debate on candidacy, as declared in the explanatory memorandum of the presidential proposal. At the same time, it cannot be overlooked that the revised project also allows groups of prosecutors to submit candidates, i.e. persons currently strictly subordinate to the legislative authority: the Minister of Justice and the Prosecutor General. Such a solution in practice means that the only judicial authority will be deprived not only of the right to elect judges to the board, which is clearly contrary to the regulation contained in the Article. 10 the Constitution and the purpose of the regulation contained in Article. 187 constitutions resulting directly from the wording of the article. 186, which states that it is up to the Council to uphold the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges, but also the real influence of judges even to identify candidates from whom the Sejm would choose. With such regulation, it is entirely possible that no candidate submitted by the judges will be even on the list of candidates established by the competent parliamentary committee. This is a complete distortion of the objectives of the presidential project and the introduction of a mechanism that denies the establishment of a judicial council as an independent body to protect the independence of the judiciary and the independence of judges and at the same time a mechanism which completely does not correspond to the standards provided for the functioning of the judiciary councils. I would like to refer here to the most recent opinion on this subject of the Executive Council of the European Judicial Councils, an opinion from yesterday which states, among other things, that: the mechanism for appointing the representatives of the judiciary to the composition of the Council must be a system which excludes any interference of the executive or legislative authority, and the choice of judges should be made only by other judges and based on a broad representation of the relevant levels of the judiciary. The standards of the European Judicial Council Network also provide that at least 50 members of the Board should be judges elected by other judges. It is clear that the choice of members of the Council by parliament, whether by ordinary or qualified majority, is not in line with the standards of the European Judicial Council Network. Other European and international standards clearly and unanimously confirm this. As I have already pointed out, it is also essential to assess the constitutionality of the proposed solution concerning the election of judges to the National Judicial Council by Parliament, as well as to shorten the term of office of the members of the Council set out directly in the Constitution. In this respect, I fully uphold the position recently adopted by the National Judicial Council in its resolution of 31 October this year. It merely requires particular emphasis that the constitutionality assessment of the choice of judges by Parliament is entirely a matter of jurisdiction and is directly linked to the interpretation of the Article. 187 constitutions. In this respect, there can be no freedom, as certain rules of interpretation apply. It is required, among others. The systemic interpretation of the provisions which does not allow for a separate interpretation of only one provision of the Constitution, and more particularly one of the paragraphs of the Constitutional Article, is a matter of Article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 4. Its meaning should be read together with the wording of the whole Article. 187, which explicitly provides for the power of Parliament to choose only four Members and two Senators. There is no regulation providing, on a presumption basis, for a wider competence of the legislative authority in this regard. It is also important to draw attention to the nature of the Article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. Four constitutions as an ordinary rule. Therefore, the legal standard, which is derived from this provision by interpretation, must, of course, be in line with the provisions of the Constitution which are of the nature of principles, such as Article. The 10th Constitution, which declares the principle of three divisions of power. It is also necessary to answer the question of the substance of the article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 4. It gives the power to clarify by law the scope of competence of the National Judicial Council by defining the way in which the Board is organised, the way in which the Board is to be dealt with and the choice of members of the Board in the selection procedure, but by the judges themselves, it can therefore be assumed that Parliament can indicate whether judges will be elected, for example. directly in elections by judges, or e.g. through some representatives, because such mixed systems of choice currently exist in the Act on the National Judicial Council. It also authorises the clarification of the rules for the representation of the different types of courts, with reference to paragraph 1. 1 point 2, so it is possible by law to determine how many will be in the Council of Representatives e.g. Supreme Court, Military Courts or Administrative Courts. There must be no doubt that this regulation does not in any way apply to the systemic plane and Article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. The 4th Constitution does not give rise to the recognition that a body entitled to elect judges may be freely designated by law. The draft that judges will elect the Sejm for the Council is therefore incompatible with the Constitution. It is also incompatible with international standards, in particular those of the Council of Europe which provide that more than half of the members of the judicial board should be judges elected by the judges themselves. It is therefore worth to refer to the resolution of the Sejm of 18 December 1991 on the accession of the Republic of Poland to the Council of Europe. Let me read this resolution because it contains only three sentences. It stresses that Poland's accession to the Council of Europe took place in a solemn manner at the 1st meeting of the Sejm elected after the free elections in 1989. And further in the resolution it was stated that the Sejm of the Republic of Poland states that it will be the guardian of democracy, freedom and civil rights. Membership in the Council of Europe is a symbol of the permanent ties of the Republic of Poland as a sovereign legal and democratic state with the community of European countries. This resolution clearly implies the will of the State to respect the Polish standards of the Council of Europe. It also needs to be stressed that the adoption of the proposed arrangements for the selection of judges to the National Council of the Court of First Instance will depreciate the full importance of the judiciary, whose powers have already been significantly reduced by depriving judges of any real impact on the cast of the positions of Presidents and Vice-Presidents of the Courts and the Directors of the Courts. Contrary to the assumptions of the justification for the presidential draft, the way in which judges are elected to the National Judicial Council, as set out in the draft, taking into account the changes made to it, will clearly lead to a politicisation of the process. Regardless of the will and real political beliefs of elected judges, they will be assigned political affiliation in the public opinion. This will have a fatal impact on the assessment of the independence of the judicial authority, as one of its guarantees is the method of appointing judges with the assistance of the other authorities of the authority, which is intended to be the National Judicial Council. The existence of such an authority belongs to international standards, in particular those developed by the Council of Europe, of which Poland has been a member since 1991. In the key issue of the independence of the judiciary of the judgment of the European Court of Human Rights in Urban and Urban v Poland, action No 23614/08, the Court stated that, in addition to the existence of guarantees of independence established by law, the question of whether the body gives the impression of independence is particularly important and therefore how this issue is assessed by citizens. The proposed changes will certainly not strengthen the social belief that the National Judicial Council is independent of the executive and legislative powers. To conclude my speech, in place of my own summary of this issue, I would like to refer to the assessment of the proposed changes contained in the previous position of the Executive Board of the European Judicial Councils Network of 5 December this year, in which it was stated that: Amendments to the Act on the National Court Register should not be seen as an isolated issue... [[Game in the O.R.]] [[Quiet.]] ...but in conjunction with the amendments made to the Law on the system of common courts and the Law on the Supreme Court. The combined treatment of recent amendments to legal acts relating to the judicial organisation seems to indicate that the executive and legislative authorities are trying to gain control of the third authority, that is, the judiciary. This is alarming and can potentially affect the position and confidence in the Polish justice system in the European legal community. The rule of law is at the heart of the European Union and is crucial for every democratic system. Respect for the rule of law is a prerequisite for protecting all fundamental values of the European Treaties, including democracy and fundamental rights. Thank you very much. I wish everyone was interested. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u523	I will now ask the Secretary of State at the Chancellery of the President of the Republic of Poland, Mr.Paweł Mucha. [[Applause]] You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u524	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Since there was a question directly related to the global and European regulations concerning the functioning of the judges' councils, but also the appointment of judges, and this was also the case in the context of the democratic system, I would like to start with these circumstances. I'm pointing out that in the United States of America, federal judges are appointed by the President on the council and consent of the Senate. Senators present their candidates, presidential candidacy is given an extremely detailed opinion by the Senate committee. State judges either appoint a governor, or they are elected by state legislatures, depending on the state. As far as the European countries are concerned, the appointing procedure has many varieties. You can distinguish three models, which can also interact with each other in a mixed model. There is a model of an autocreation system when the judge's environment plays a dominant role in the nomination process. There is also a model, quite common in Europe, which would contradict the thesis that has been put here - that in such a case, if that is the case, we are not dealing with a democratic state - exclusive in this respect enforcement. This is a situation in which only the executive authority is granted the right to form the personal composition of general court judges. There are such democracies in Europe where only enforcement is responsible for the personal composition of universal courts. There are countries in which we are dealing with a mixed model, which assumes participation in the nomination process of at least two entities coming from different segments of power, the judge's environment, the legislature or enforcement. In Europe, in eight countries, there is a model of self-creation that assumes the dominance of the judicial community in the appointment of judges, while in seven other countries the appointments of judges are solely the competence of enforcement. That's the way it is. in the Czech Republic. In 12 other countries, at least two actors are expected to work together - judicial councils with enforcement or legislation, respectively. In Norway, for example, we have the interaction of enforcement with the judicial council, in Slovenia, the cooperation of the legislative with the judicial council, a similar model is in the UK. The judges' councils also function in very different dimensions - either as self-government or as professional organisations consisting of judges or judges and lawyers. The prototype of the Polish solution, the first one, in the case of the judges' council, was the French High Council of the judiciary, which was not only chaired by the President of the Republic, but also appointed by nine members. In neighbouring countries such as Romania, Bulgaria, members respectively... [[But he doesn't choose.]] ...the judges' council chooses a parliament or a parliament and local judges' organizations. [[He calls, he doesn't choose.]] So, ladies and gentlemen, first of all, I must point out that the choice of judges by judges on the basis of the article. 187 of the Constitution was a constitutional principle. There is no such provision in the Polish Constitution. Art. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 4 provides: The system, scope and mode of operation of the National Judicial Board and the way in which its members are elected shall be governed by the law. The Act establishes these powers. The legislator will settle this. These powers will be defined by the law. On the other hand, as regards the appointment of judges in this model: only enforcement, it cannot be accepted that such states are not democracies. The model presented by your President is also a reference to those solutions that are in the Spanish model. We do not deal with such a situation in the Polish legal system that the National Council of Courts is to be regarded only as a judicial authority, and this is a mixed body which combines the powers of the authority, which is a specific body of state authority, and certain elements related to the representation of the judge. The provision of the Article is crucial. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 4. Hence, I do not share these voices, as it was pointed out, that such a way of choosing constitutes a violation of the principles of a democratic rule of law or a violation of the Constitution of 2 April 1997. What's more, the views of the Poles I was talking about... The legitimacy of judges who will sit on the National Council of Courts - this is not in doubt for us - will be much greater when judges will not be elected by judges, but when they will be elected by representatives of the nation, i.e. Members of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. [[Members of the Law and Justice, Minister.]] Please note that today we are dealing with such a situation that the main determinant of promotion is how the judicial community evaluates the judge concerned. What an assessment is formulated by the judicial community determines whether someone is going from a regional court to a district court or an appeal court. And is such a model in which judges judge without any social control appropriate? We don't think it's right, it's wrong. [[Applause]] It is so that the choice of judges as members of the National Council of Courts by the Polish Sejm is the introduction of a social control factor, it is also the introduction of appeal to the principle of sovereignty of the nation. There were voices here that the constitutional provisions should not be interpreted in alienation, abstracting from other provisions - the introduction to the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, art. 4, the principle of sovereignty of the nation, Members of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland as representatives of the nation. Ladies and gentlemen, it would be possible by analogy to talk about situations where a corporation or quasi-corporation has decided on certain issues relating to the organisation of certain spheres. I can give you an example of the corporate exam at the start of legal applications, the situation of law change and the introduction of the state exam. We can ask such an open question: Is it better when only lawyers or lawyers have decided to apply for legal applications, or is it better when there is a public examination and objective criteria for this selection? [[Applause]] So let's take a look at the analogy that goes straight here: Is it better when there is a choice of judges by judges, or when we hand it over to the people? And the representatives of the nation are Members of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. I do not share those reservations which were formulated as regards the constitutionality of the proposed law. As we originally pointed out, we ask the High House to support this presidential project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u533	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. As the first Mr Rafał Weber, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u534	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! By representing the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I have the honour to present our opinion on the draft law on public roads, a project prepared by the government and contained in print no. 2038. The project involves two fundamental changes. One concerns that municipal and district roads may also be included in the category of defensive roads. In the current state of law, only national roads and part of voivodship roads may be of such a nature, whereas the amendment proposed by the Ministry is that ministers, the Minister of Defence, in agreement with the Minister responsible for transport, may attach district roads or municipal roads to the list of defensive roads. Ministers will set these paths by means of an order which will not be announced, and this legal status also applies in this situation. The argument behind this change is that, after all, right now, and in the past, and probably in the future Polish army, the Polish army uses and will use these kinds of roads to carry out its activities, to move to transport soldiers even on the ground, to transport the equipment needed to carry out activities, i.e. to conduct exercises. And it's obvious that this type of maneuvering, this kind of operation of these roads, causes them to be destroyed. The inclusion of these roads in defence roads will also be linked to the responsibility of the Ministry of National Defence to finance these roads. So that one change: the inclusion of municipal and district roads by means of an order issued by national defence ministers and the minister responsible for transport. And the second point is to fund the financing tasks of these roads. This matter, in my view, in the judgment of the Parliamentary Club, is not a matter of any concern. The one who uses, the one who exploits, the one who in a sense destroys, because every route used sooner or later is destroyed, should pay, should finance renovations, upgrades or bring these roads to a state of use. In our view, High Izbo, this regulation does not raise any doubts. I am surprised that the opposition is in doubt that the opposition has sought to delete this provision and that this provision does not exist. Imagine a situation in which the municipal road is destroyed by military equipment, and the uncle has to fix this road with local money, which is very often modest. In my opinion, it shouldn't be like that. We are fighting - the opposition is also considered to be fighting for local governments - so that every task imposed on them also has government funding. That's what we want to do in this case. The municipal road or district road, which will be included in defence roads, will also be maintained by the Ministry of National Defence. We believe that this solution is right and right, and we will urge that this solution be included in this proposal. In conclusion, Law and Justice support this government submission. We believe that this is a project needed, that this is a project that has the justification that this is a project necessary, that this is a good change in the approach to defence infrastructure and that this is a change that will strengthen our security and will be one of the elements just strengthening the defence potential of the Republic of Poland. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-06-sejm-53-1.u575	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I present my position on behalf of Kol Poselskiego Free and Solidarni on the government draft law amending the Public Road Act. The need to ensure security is one of the most important obligations of the state towards its citizens. In order to enable the tasks of the Polish Army to be carried out, it is necessary to prepare and maintain the infrastructure for transport. The proposed law will determine the status of defence roads and the improved investment process. The changes will give us legal provisions allowing us to make investments from the state budget on roads of significant communication and at the same time defence importance. Paragraph 2 of the proposed law provides that not only public roads, but also individual major sections of public roads, will be specified by the Minister of Infrastructure issued in agreement with the Minister of Defence. So far, only national and voivodship roads have been included in the category of roads known as defence roads, unfortunately this did not conform to the demands of the Armed Forces. The State's needs for defence point to the need to use, apart from national and voivodship roads, other categories of roads for this purpose. District roads, municipal roads or their sections considered necessary to provide the Armed Forces with access, among others. to airports, seaports, crossings, yards, material warehouses and special facilities are not legally classified as defensive roads. Points 3 and 4 of the proposed law regulate this. Paragraph 6 regulates competence issues. It is proposed that the GDDKiA prepares the national roads of which it is the manager for the defence of the State, while for other public roads, i.e. national roads in cities on the rights of the county - excluding express roads and motorways - voivodship, district and communal roads, GDDKiA will supervise the preparation of these roads for the defence of the State. In addition, the Minister of Infrastructure, in agreement with the Minister for National Defence, shall determine the routes and implement technical and defence requirements for the preparation of networks and road facilities used for the defence of the State. Paragraph 7 of the draft law provides that defence-related road tasks carried out in the framework of the defence preparations of the State are financed by the State budget in the framework of defence expenditure. In conclusion, the amendments proposed in the Public Roads Act will allow the use for State defence of all categories of public roads or sections necessary to safeguard the transport needs of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland and NATO. These changes are necessary to ensure the proper security of the State. In view of the above, the Free and Solidarni Circle will vote in favour of the adoption of a government bill amending the Public Road Act.
